### 2d19370c7e — 037_turn_of_month_fade_tf15_time (BTCUSDT 15m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\037_turn_of_month_fade_tf15_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Month-end and month-start are calendar events, not price events. Institutional risk budgets reset, index products and mandates rebalance, and benchmark-tracking funds must sell what fell and buy what rose in order to return to their weights - all of it in the last days of a month and the first days of the next, without any view on BTC. That creates a directional push that ends on a date, which is a testable asymmetry rather than a mood: the flow that pushed price into the month boundary is the flow that stops at it. So this idea fades the move that runs into a month boundary and is a natural both-sided trade - it is long after a drop into the window and short after a rise into it, which is why it is the only idea left that is structurally incapable of being a disguised long, and why the round's beta requirement is the real test of it. The counterparty is the rebalancing flow itself, and it is calendar-driven, so the effect should be present in every year rather than tracking the market's direction. [Timeframe variant of 037_turn_of_month_fade, 240m -> 15m, mode time: bar counts x16, ATR multiples x4.000, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 0 with ≥100 train trades, n/a of those positive on train

### 45e51d6f8d — 038_opening_range_both_sides_tf15_time (BTCUSDT 15m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\038_opening_range_both_sides_tf15_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A bug correction, not a new hypothesis. R4.2's 00:00 UTC opening-range break returned a (long, short) tuple where every trigger returns one signed array, so `recipe()` read 'either side fired' as LONG and the whole of Exp 022 ran long-only: 798 long trades and 0 short. Its control verdicts (DRIFT, NO_EDGE) stand and the round's conclusion is unchanged, but the structure itself was never actually tested on both sides, and it was the only Round 4 structure whose gross move exceeded the 0.11% cost line (+0.141% of price at 4h). The 00:00 print resets positioning once a day - brackets are rebuilt around it - so its first hour is a level with order flow behind it, and a close outside it is the first moment those brackets are all on the wrong side. This re-test adds what Round 4 did not have: a 6% stop and a 96h hold, so the gross move is measured against a cost of 0.043 R rather than 0.15 R. [Timeframe variant of 038_opening_range_both_sides, 240m -> 15m, mode time: bar counts x16, ATR multiples x4.000, same hours and % stops.]

### 861747899d — 033_funding_carry_tf5_time (BTCUSDT 5m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\033_funding_carry_tf5_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Funding is not a fee, it is a transfer: every eight hours the longs pay the shorts, at a rate set by the imbalance between the two sides' leverage. When the rate is extreme, the paying side is the crowded side, and the reason it pays is that its positions are too big to unwind at once. That is the mechanism this idea trades: not a price reversal but a position-size imbalance, whose unwinding takes days rather than hours. Take the paying side's side - short when longs are paying - and the position earns the funding while it waits. Idea 003 tried this at 15m with a 12h hold and produced 13 trades, so the hypothesis has never really been tested at a horizon where the unwind can happen. Short-only in practice on this sample (154 TRAIN signals, 108 of them short), and the long/short split is reported separately because a carry idea that is secretly one-directional is a drift idea. [Timeframe variant of 033_funding_carry, 240m -> 5m, mode time: bar counts x48, ATR multiples x6.928, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 2 with ≥100 train trades, 100% of those positive on train

### fc8f47c2f5 — 033_funding_carry_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\033_funding_carry_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Funding is not a fee, it is a transfer: every eight hours the longs pay the shorts, at a rate set by the imbalance between the two sides' leverage. When the rate is extreme, the paying side is the crowded side, and the reason it pays is that its positions are too big to unwind at once. That is the mechanism this idea trades: not a price reversal but a position-size imbalance, whose unwinding takes days rather than hours. Take the paying side's side - short when longs are paying - and the position earns the funding while it waits. Idea 003 tried this at 15m with a 12h hold and produced 13 trades, so the hypothesis has never really been tested at a horizon where the unwind can happen. Short-only in practice on this sample (154 TRAIN signals, 108 of them short), and the long/short split is reported separately because a carry idea that is secretly one-directional is a drift idea. [Timeframe variant of 033_funding_carry, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]

### 64c3663313 — 034_multiday_reversal_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\034_multiday_reversal_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Over five to ten days a 2-sigma move is not information, it is a position: the move is long enough that trend followers have added to it and leveraged accounts have been funded into it, and both need out at the same time. The counterparty is therefore not a view but a margin call, and margin calls unwind on the timeframe of days, not hours - which is why this was never visible at the 12h horizons where mean reversion was tested and rejected. The edge, if there is one, is that a stretched move overshoots because forced selling does not stop at a fair price, and part of the overshoot is handed back as the forced sellers become the buyers. This is the one reversion idea the project has not closed, and the difference from Exp 016's is the horizon, not the signal. ### 8513bae360 — 035_multiday_momentum_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\035_multiday_momentum_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A five to twenty day high or low is where trend-following funds add risk, because their mandate is to hold the extreme rather than to forecast it. That is a structural reason for the move to continue rather than reverse: the buyers of a new high are institutions with a rule that says buy, not people with a view. The reason to test it both-sided with no directional filter is that this effect should not care about the sign of the market - it continued down through 2022 and up through 2023-24, and a rule that only works in the bull market is the drift this project has spent four rounds trying to separate from an edge. The kill condition is deliberately two-sided: it dies if TRAIN gross_r <= 0, and it also dies if the whole result is one side in the period where that side matches the drift. [Timeframe variant of 035_multiday_momentum, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]

### 640ca1c1f1 — 036_keltner_multiday_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 02:20 UTC · idea `ideas\036_keltner_multiday_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Idea 032 put a Keltner break on a 12h hold with an htf_trend filter, and produced Round 4's largest TRAIN gross - and was DRIFT, which is the pattern of the whole round: a real move at a horizon where cost eats it, or a filter that makes a both-sided idea long. This idea removes both problems at once. The channel is a volatility band, so it widens when price has been moving and narrows when it has not, and its break therefore means price has left a band whose width is itself a measure of movement. Held for days with a 6% stop, the gross move it captures is several times the 0.11% cost line, and with no directional filter at all it cannot be a disguised long. The pairs gridded are the two that survive constraint 5: `mult` is fixed at 2.0 because (n 20, mult 3.0) produced only 62 TRAIN signals and was dropped before running. ### 87dae576c8 — 037_turn_of_month_fade_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 02:21 UTC · idea `ideas\037_turn_of_month_fade_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Month-end and month-start are calendar events, not price events. Institutional risk budgets reset, index products and mandates rebalance, and benchmark-tracking funds must sell what fell and buy what rose in order to return to their weights - all of it in the last days of a month and the first days of the next, without any view on BTC. That creates a directional push that ends on a date, which is a testable asymmetry rather than a mood: the flow that pushed price into the month boundary is the flow that stops at it. So this idea fades the move that runs into a month boundary and is a natural both-sided trade - it is long after a drop into the window and short after a rise into it, which is why it is the only idea left that is structurally incapable of being a disguised long, and why the round's beta requirement is the real test of it. The counterparty is the rebalancing flow itself, and it is calendar-driven, so the effect should be present in every year rather than tracking the market's direction. [Timeframe variant of 037_turn_of_month_fade, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]

- 2026-09-29 02:21 UTC · idea `ideas\035_multiday_momentum_tf1_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A five to twenty day high or low is where trend-following funds add risk, because their mandate is to hold the extreme rather than to forecast it. The reason to test it both-sided with no directional filter is that this effect should not care about the sign of the market - it continued down through 2022 and up through 2023-24, and a rule that only works in the bull market is the drift this project has spent four rounds trying to separate from an edge. The kill condition is deliberately two-sided: it dies if TRAIN gross_r <= 0, and it also dies if the whole result is one side in the period where that side matches the drift. ### 83a8faa8d6 — 037_turn_of_month_fade_tf1_time (BTCUSDT 1m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 02:22 UTC · idea `ideas\037_turn_of_month_fade_tf1_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Month-end and month-start are calendar events, not price events. Institutional risk budgets reset, index products and mandates rebalance, and benchmark-tracking funds must sell what fell and buy what rose in order to return to their weights - all of it in the last days of a month and the first days of the next, without any view on BTC. That creates a directional push that ends on a date, which is a testable asymmetry rather than a mood: the flow that pushed price into the month boundary is the flow that stops at it. So this idea fades the move that runs into a month boundary and is a natural both-sided trade - it is long after a drop into the window and short after a rise into it, which is why it is the only idea left that is structurally incapable of being a disguised long, and why the round's beta requirement is the real test of it. The counterparty is the rebalancing flow itself, and it is calendar-driven, so the effect should be present in every year rather than tracking the market's direction. [Timeframe variant of 037_turn_of_month_fade, 240m -> 1m, mode time: bar counts x240, ATR multiples x15.492, same hours and % stops.]

### 47015e7aec — 039_breakout_flow_confirm (BTCUSDT 240m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\039_breakout_flow_confirm.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A breakout is only new positioning if aggressive traders keep paying for it. A close through the n-bar high on above-average volume says many participants moved; the taker buy ratio says which side paid to move it. When both agree, the move is buyers arriving and the sellers who were defending the level are the ones who have to cover, and covering is a forced, price-insensitive order over the next days. The other side is resting liquidity and short-term faders, who are the liquidity this trade takes. A breakout without that flow is a different animal: it is a stop run through a thin book, it fills the breakout traders rather than the breakout traders' targets, and it reverses. That is why these are one idea and not two filters - flow without participation is a single trader getting filled, and participation without directional flow is a market-wide move nobody is fighting. Both directions, no trend filter, held for days.
- grid: 4 combos, 4 with ≥100 train trades, 0% of those positive on train

### 375ed0efd9 — 040_stop_hunt_trap (BTCUSDT 240m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\040_stop_hunt_trap.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Two blocks in this project have never been tested together. `failed_break` says price pierced a multi-day extreme and closed back inside, which leaves the breakout traders wrong and needing to cover; on its own at a 12h hold it had a negative gross, because the reclaim has already happened by the time the signal fires. `flush` says a liquidation-sized bar followed, which on its own also had no gross edge in either direction. Together they are a different event, not a stricter version of either: the pierce is the trap being sprung and the large bar is the cascade that cleared the people it was sprung on, and whoever was on the other side of that cascade now holds a materially better price than the ones who chased. The sequence has to be an order, not a coincidence, so this requires both triggers inside three bars of each other and the same direction. Both sides, no trend filter, held for days.

### c4de803f1d — 041_impulse_not_crowded (BTCUSDT 240m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\041_impulse_not_crowded.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Momentum works until it is crowded, and funding is the only place you can see the crowding before you join it. A strong multi-bar impulse on real volume is a real move, but if funding is already stretched in the impulse's own direction then the leveraged money that pushed it is already there, and the trade's counterparty is not faders who will cover - it is the same leveraged cohort that will be liquidated the moment the move pauses, which is what makes the late joiner the exit liquidity. So this takes the impulse only when funding says the crowd is not on that side, plus volume to confirm participation. Funding is used as a signal here and never as a return: the engine still pays and receives it as a cost on every trade (Exp 026 put carry strategies out of scope). The kill condition has two halves on purpose: a result carried entirely by one side is the drift this project has spent five rounds separating from an edge.

### 4ad580b5b0 — 042_trend_start_three_ways (BTCUSDT 240m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\042_trend_start_three_ways.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Each trend indicator fails on its own noise: an EMA cross whipsaws inside a range, a Supertrend flip whipsaws in chop, and +DI/-DI disagrees with both during the turn. The three do not fail on the same bars, because they are computed from different things - a moving average of closes, an ATR-based band, and the directional movement index - so requiring all three to point the same way inside three days is a much stricter statement than any of them alone, and the bars it rejects are mostly bars where one indicator was late rather than wrong. Rounds 1-2 tested these blocks separately and mostly long; this is the first test that lets both sides trade them, at a multi-day hold where a trend that has actually started has time to pay. The counterparty is the fader who read the same three indicators at different times and is now on the wrong side of a move that three of them agree on. Grid covers exits only: `confirm_bars` 6 and 12 give 132 and 145 TRAIN signals, under the 150 floor, so 18 is fixed and the grid sweeps the stop and the hold.

### 1a0855b63b — 043_squeeze_multiday_break (BTCUSDT 240m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\043_squeeze_multiday_break.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Volatility clusters, and the mechanism for that is positions: in a quiet range, leverage is cheap and positions accumulate against a range that is not moving, so when something pushes price out of the range those positions all have to be closed at once and the move that follows is larger than the move that started it. A Bollinger squeeze is the observable version of that quiet range and a Donchian break is the observable version of the push, so the combination is a claim about mechanism rather than a stricter version of either block. The earlier test of this pair in this project (004, 013) had a trend filter and 12 h holds, which means it never tested the break at all - it tested the filter. This one has neither, and holds for days, which is the horizon over which a volatility expansion actually pays. `volume_spike` is left out on purpose: with it, n 20 gives only 103-122 TRAIN signals.

### 482c7d89b1 — 041_impulse_not_crowded_tf60_time (BTCUSDT 60m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\041_impulse_not_crowded_tf60_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Momentum works until it is crowded, and funding is the only place you can see the crowding before you join it. A strong multi-bar impulse on real volume is a real move, but if funding is already stretched in the impulse's own direction then the leveraged money that pushed it is already there, and the trade's counterparty is not faders who will cover - it is the same leveraged cohort that will be liquidated the moment the move pauses, which is what makes the late joiner the exit liquidity. So this takes the impulse only when funding says the crowd is not on that side, plus volume to confirm participation. Funding is used as a signal here and never as a return: the engine still pays and receives it as a cost on every trade (Exp 026 put carry strategies out of scope). The kill condition has two halves on purpose: a result carried entirely by one side is the drift this project has spent five rounds separating from an edge. [Timeframe variant of 041_impulse_not_crowded, 240m -> 60m, mode time: bar counts x4, ATR multiples x2.000, same hours and % stops.]

### fed46be9a9 — 042_trend_start_three_ways_tf60_time (BTCUSDT 60m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\042_trend_start_three_ways_tf60_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Each trend indicator fails on its own noise: an EMA cross whipsaws inside a range, a Supertrend flip whipsaws in chop, and +DI/-DI disagrees with both during the turn. The three do not fail on the same bars, because they are computed from different things - a moving average of closes, an ATR-based band, and the directional movement index - so requiring all three to point the same way inside three days is a much stricter statement than any of them alone, and the bars it rejects are mostly bars where one indicator was late rather than wrong. Rounds 1-2 tested these blocks separately and mostly long; this is the first test that lets both sides trade them, at a multi-day hold where a trend that has actually started has time to pay. The counterparty is the fader who read the same three indicators at different times and is now on the wrong side of a move that three of them agree on. Grid covers exits only: `confirm_bars` 6 and 12 give 132 and 145 TRAIN signals, under the 150 floor, so 18 is fixed and the grid sweeps the stop and the hold. [Timeframe variant of 042_trend_start_three_ways, 240m -> 60m, mode time: bar counts x4, ATR multiples x2.000, same hours and % stops.]

### 771bf45117 — 043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf60_time (BTCUSDT 60m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:17 UTC · idea `ideas\043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf60_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Volatility clusters, and the mechanism for that is positions: in a quiet range, leverage is cheap and positions accumulate against a range that is not moving, so when something pushes price out of the range those positions all have to be closed at once and the move that follows is larger than the move that started it. A Bollinger squeeze is the observable version of that quiet range and a Donchian break is the observable version of the push, so the combination is a claim about mechanism rather than a stricter version of either block. The earlier test of this pair in this project (004, 013) had a trend filter and 12 h holds, which means it never tested the break at all - it tested the filter. This one has neither, and holds for days, which is the horizon over which a volatility expansion actually pays. `volume_spike` is left out on purpose: with it, n 20 gives only 103-122 TRAIN signals. [Timeframe variant of 043_squeeze_multiday_break, 240m -> 60m, mode time: bar counts x4, ATR multiples x2.000, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 3 with ≥100 train trades, 67% of those positive on train

- 2026-09-29 05:18 UTC · idea `ideas\043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf30_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Volatility clusters, and the mechanism for that is positions: in a quiet range, leverage is cheap and positions accumulate against a range that is not moving, so when something pushes price out of the range those positions all have to be closed at once and the move that follows is larger than the move that started it. A Bollinger squeeze is the observable version of that quiet range and a Donchian break is the observable version of the push, so the combination is a claim about mechanism rather than a stricter version of either block. The earlier test of this pair in this project (004, 013) had a trend filter and 12 h holds, which means it never tested the break at all - it tested the filter. This one has neither, and holds for days, which is the horizon over which a volatility expansion actually pays. `volume_spike` is left out on purpose: with it, n 20 gives only 103-122 TRAIN signals. ### 99c26dc455 — 039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf15_time (BTCUSDT 15m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:18 UTC · idea `ideas\039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf15_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A breakout is only new positioning if aggressive traders keep paying for it. A close through the n-bar high on above-average volume says many participants moved; the taker buy ratio says which side paid to move it. When both agree, the move is buyers arriving and the sellers who were defending the level are the ones who have to cover, and covering is a forced, price-insensitive order over the next days. The other side is resting liquidity and short-term faders, who are the liquidity this trade takes. A breakout without that flow is a different animal: it is a stop run through a thin book, it fills the breakout traders rather than the breakout traders' targets, and it reverses. That is why these are one idea and not two filters - flow without participation is a single trader getting filled, and participation without directional flow is a market-wide move nobody is fighting. Both directions, no trend filter, held for days. [Timeframe variant of 039_breakout_flow_confirm, 240m -> 15m, mode time: bar counts x16, ATR multiples x4.000, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 4 with ≥100 train trades, 50% of those positive on train

- hypothesis: Two blocks in this project have never been tested together. `failed_break` says price pierced a multi-day extreme and closed back inside, which leaves the breakout traders wrong and needing to cover; on its own at a 12h hold it had a negative gross, because the reclaim has already happened by the time the signal fires. `flush` says a liquidation-sized bar followed, which on its own also had no gross edge in either direction. ### 85aa125959 — 041_impulse_not_crowded_tf15_time (BTCUSDT 15m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:18 UTC · idea `ideas\041_impulse_not_crowded_tf15_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Momentum works until it is crowded, and funding is the only place you can see the crowding before you join it. A strong multi-bar impulse on real volume is a real move, but if funding is already stretched in the impulse's own direction then the leveraged money that pushed it is already there, and the trade's counterparty is not faders who will cover - it is the same leveraged cohort that will be liquidated the moment the move pauses, which is what makes the late joiner the exit liquidity. So this takes the impulse only when funding says the crowd is not on that side, plus volume to confirm participation. Funding is used as a signal here and never as a return: the engine still pays and receives it as a cost on every trade (Exp 026 put carry strategies out of scope). The kill condition has two halves on purpose: a result carried entirely by one side is the drift this project has spent five rounds separating from an edge. [Timeframe variant of 041_impulse_not_crowded, 240m -> 15m, mode time: bar counts x16, ATR multiples x4.000, same hours and % stops.]

### 274c0c747a — 042_trend_start_three_ways_tf15_time (BTCUSDT 15m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 05:18 UTC · idea `ideas\042_trend_start_three_ways_tf15_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Each trend indicator fails on its own noise: an EMA cross whipsaws inside a range, a Supertrend flip whipsaws in chop, and +DI/-DI disagrees with both during the turn. The three do not fail on the same bars, because they are computed from different things - a moving average of closes, an ATR-based band, and the directional movement index - so requiring all three to point the same way inside three days is a much stricter statement than any of them alone, and the bars it rejects are mostly bars where one indicator was late rather than wrong. Rounds 1-2 tested these blocks separately and mostly long; this is the first test that lets both sides trade them, at a multi-day hold where a trend that has actually started has time to pay. The counterparty is the fader who read the same three indicators at different times and is now on the wrong side of a move that three of them agree on. Grid covers exits only: `confirm_bars` 6 and 12 give 132 and 145 TRAIN signals, under the 150 floor, so 18 is fixed and the grid sweeps the stop and the hold. [Timeframe variant of 042_trend_start_three_ways, 240m -> 15m, mode time: bar counts x16, ATR multiples x4.000, same hours and % stops.]

### 1b164063da — 043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf5_time (BTCUSDT 5m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:18 UTC · idea `ideas\043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf5_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Volatility clusters, and the mechanism for that is positions: in a quiet range, leverage is cheap and positions accumulate against a range that is not moving, so when something pushes price out of the range those positions all have to be closed at once and the move that follows is larger than the move that started it. A Bollinger squeeze is the observable version of that quiet range and a Donchian break is the observable version of the push, so the combination is a claim about mechanism rather than a stricter version of either block. The earlier test of this pair in this project (004, 013) had a trend filter and 12 h holds, which means it never tested the break at all - it tested the filter. This one has neither, and holds for days, which is the horizon over which a volatility expansion actually pays. `volume_spike` is left out on purpose: with it, n 20 gives only 103-122 TRAIN signals. [Timeframe variant of 043_squeeze_multiday_break, 240m -> 5m, mode time: bar counts x48, ATR multiples x6.928, same hours and % stops.]

### b5b91270e2 — 039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:19 UTC · idea `ideas\039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A breakout is only new positioning if aggressive traders keep paying for it. A close through the n-bar high on above-average volume says many participants moved; the taker buy ratio says which side paid to move it. When both agree, the move is buyers arriving and the sellers who were defending the level are the ones who have to cover, and covering is a forced, price-insensitive order over the next days. The other side is resting liquidity and short-term faders, who are the liquidity this trade takes. A breakout without that flow is a different animal: it is a stop run through a thin book, it fills the breakout traders rather than the breakout traders' targets, and it reverses. That is why these are one idea and not two filters - flow without participation is a single trader getting filled, and participation without directional flow is a market-wide move nobody is fighting. Both directions, no trend filter, held for days. [Timeframe variant of 039_breakout_flow_confirm, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 4 with ≥100 train trades, 0% of those positive on train

### a0324e8336 — 040_stop_hunt_trap_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:19 UTC · idea `ideas\040_stop_hunt_trap_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Two blocks in this project have never been tested together. `failed_break` says price pierced a multi-day extreme and closed back inside, which leaves the breakout traders wrong and needing to cover; on its own at a 12h hold it had a negative gross, because the reclaim has already happened by the time the signal fires. `flush` says a liquidation-sized bar followed, which on its own also had no gross edge in either direction. Together they are a different event, not a stricter version of either: the pierce is the trap being sprung and the large bar is the cascade that cleared the people it was sprung on, and whoever was on the other side of that cascade now holds a materially better price than the ones who chased. The sequence has to be an order, not a coincidence, so this requires both triggers inside three bars of each other and the same direction. Both sides, no trend filter, held for days. [Timeframe variant of 040_stop_hunt_trap, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]

### f458e991b6 — 041_impulse_not_crowded_tf3_time (BTCUSDT 3m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:19 UTC · idea `ideas\041_impulse_not_crowded_tf3_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Momentum works until it is crowded, and funding is the only place you can see the crowding before you join it. A strong multi-bar impulse on real volume is a real move, but if funding is already stretched in the impulse's own direction then the leveraged money that pushed it is already there, and the trade's counterparty is not faders who will cover - it is the same leveraged cohort that will be liquidated the moment the move pauses, which is what makes the late joiner the exit liquidity. So this takes the impulse only when funding says the crowd is not on that side, plus volume to confirm participation. Funding is used as a signal here and never as a return: the engine still pays and receives it as a cost on every trade (Exp 026 put carry strategies out of scope). The kill condition has two halves on purpose: a result carried entirely by one side is the drift this project has spent five rounds separating from an edge. [Timeframe variant of 041_impulse_not_crowded, 240m -> 3m, mode time: bar counts x80, ATR multiples x8.944, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 2 with ≥100 train trades, 100% of those positive on train

### d613321f73 — 041_impulse_not_crowded_tf1_time (BTCUSDT 1m) — **WATCH**

- 2026-09-29 05:20 UTC · idea `ideas\041_impulse_not_crowded_tf1_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Momentum works until it is crowded, and funding is the only place you can see the crowding before you join it. A strong multi-bar impulse on real volume is a real move, but if funding is already stretched in the impulse's own direction then the leveraged money that pushed it is already there, and the trade's counterparty is not faders who will cover - it is the same leveraged cohort that will be liquidated the moment the move pauses, which is what makes the late joiner the exit liquidity. So this takes the impulse only when funding says the crowd is not on that side, plus volume to confirm participation. Funding is used as a signal here and never as a return: the engine still pays and receives it as a cost on every trade (Exp 026 put carry strategies out of scope). The kill condition has two halves on purpose: a result carried entirely by one side is the drift this project has spent five rounds separating from an edge. [Timeframe variant of 041_impulse_not_crowded, 240m -> 1m, mode time: bar counts x240, ATR multiples x15.492, same hours and % stops.]
- grid: 4 combos, 2 with ≥100 train trades, 100% of those positive on train

### b00973cf99 — 042_trend_start_three_ways_tf1_time (BTCUSDT 1m) — **INCONCLUSIVE**

- 2026-09-29 05:20 UTC · idea `ideas\042_trend_start_three_ways_tf1_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Each trend indicator fails on its own noise: an EMA cross whipsaws inside a range, a Supertrend flip whipsaws in chop, and +DI/-DI disagrees with both during the turn. The three do not fail on the same bars, because they are computed from different things - a moving average of closes, an ATR-based band, and the directional movement index - so requiring all three to point the same way inside three days is a much stricter statement than any of them alone, and the bars it rejects are mostly bars where one indicator was late rather than wrong. Rounds 1-2 tested these blocks separately and mostly long; this is the first test that lets both sides trade them, at a multi-day hold where a trend that has actually started has time to pay. The counterparty is the fader who read the same three indicators at different times and is now on the wrong side of a move that three of them agree on. Grid covers exits only: `confirm_bars` 6 and 12 give 132 and 145 TRAIN signals, under the 150 floor, so 18 is fixed and the grid sweeps the stop and the hold. [Timeframe variant of 042_trend_start_three_ways, 240m -> 1m, mode time: bar counts x240, ATR multiples x15.492, same hours and % stops.]

### e57da53438 — 043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf1_time (BTCUSDT 1m) — **REJECT**

- 2026-09-29 05:21 UTC · idea `ideas\043_squeeze_multiday_break_tf1_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: Volatility clusters, and the mechanism for that is positions: in a quiet range, leverage is cheap and positions accumulate against a range that is not moving, so when something pushes price out of the range those positions all have to be closed at once and the move that follows is larger than the move that started it. A Bollinger squeeze is the observable version of that quiet range and a Donchian break is the observable version of the push, so the combination is a claim about mechanism rather than a stricter version of either block. The earlier test of this pair in this project (004, 013) had a trend filter and 12 h holds, which means it never tested the break at all - it tested the filter. This one has neither, and holds for days, which is the horizon over which a volatility expansion actually pays. `volume_spike` is left out on purpose: with it, n 20 gives only 103-122 TRAIN signals. [Timeframe variant of 043_squeeze_multiday_break, 240m -> 1m, mode time: bar counts x240, ATR multiples x15.492, same hours and % stops.]

### c579c4e873 — 039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf5_time (BTCUSDT 5m) — **PASS**

- 2026-09-29 05:51 UTC · idea `ideas\039_breakout_flow_confirm_tf5_time.json` · strategy `recipe`
- hypothesis: A breakout is only new positioning if aggressive traders keep paying for it. A close through the n-bar high on above-average volume says many participants moved; the taker buy ratio says which side paid to move it. When both agree, the move is buyers arriving and the sellers who were defending the level are the ones who have to cover, and covering is a forced, price-insensitive order over the next days. The other side is resting liquidity and short-term faders, who are the liquidity this trade takes. A breakout without that flow is a different animal: it is a stop run through a thin book, it fills the breakout traders rather than the breakout traders' targets, and it reverses. That is why these are one idea and not two filters - flow without participation is a single trader getting filled, and participation without directional flow is a market-wide move nobody is fighting. Both directions, no trend filter, held for days. [Timeframe variant of 039_breakout_flow_confirm, 240m -> 5m, mode time: bar counts x48, ATR multiples x6.928, same hours and % stops.]
